Here's a summary of the project:

**ccs-viewer** — a Rust CLI tool (v0.11.0) for viewing/parsing Claude Code session files (JSONL format).

**Structure:**
- `src/main.rs`, `src/lib.rs`, `src/types.rs` — core source
- `data/` — test data (JSONL files)
- `notes/` — task tracking and chore logs
- Uses `serde`/`serde_json` for deserialization, `clap` for CLI, `glob` for file matching

**Recent history** — rapid feature development from 0.5.0 to 0.11.0, adding CLI flags, agent session support, compact output, and unknown field handling.

**Open tasks:**
- Fix remaining deserialization errors across ~5082 files
- Refactor common metadata into a shared `SessionMetadata` struct

**Dual-repo setup** — app repo at root, bot session repo at `.claude/`, both managed with `jj`.

id: toolu_01WkK7bFLYz8rMdjVeUKvJGG
```
     1→# Notes
     2→
     3→This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
     4→Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
     5→
     6→## CLI Usage
     7→
     8→```
     9→ccs-viewer [OPTIONS] <PATTERNS>...
    10→```
    11→
    12→By default, only the summary line is printed. Use flags for more detail.
    13→
    14→### Options
    15→
    16→| Flag | Short | Description |
    17→|------|-------|-------------|
    18→| `--list` | `-l` | Show per-file summary lines |
    19→| `--errors` | `-e` | Show grouped error details after summary |
    20→| `--recursive` | `-r` | Treat positional args as directories, search recursively |
    21→| `--glob <PAT>` | | File pattern for recursive mode (repeatable, default: `*.jsonl`, `*.meta.json`) |
    22→| `--version` | `-V` | Print version |
    23→| `--help` | `-h` | Print help |
    24→
    25→### Positional arguments
    26→
    27→Without `-r`: file glob patterns expanded by the program.
    28→With `-r`: directory paths (or directory globs) to search recursively.
    29→
    30→### Examples
    31→
    32→```
    33→# Summary only (default)
    34→ccs-viewer "data/*.jsonl"
    35→
    36→# Per-file list + summary
    37→ccs-viewer -l "data/*.jsonl"
    38→
    39→# Summary + error details
    40→ccs-viewer -e "data/*.jsonl"
    41→
    42→# Recursive search in a directory
    43→ccs-viewer -r .claude
    44→
    45→# Recursive with custom file patterns
    46→ccs-viewer -r --glob "*.jsonl" --glob "*.json" .claude
    47→
    48→# Multiple directories
    49→ccs-viewer -r .claude ../vc-x1/.claude
    50→
    51→# Directory glob
    52→ccs-viewer -r "../*/.claude"
    53→
    54→# All flags
    55→ccs-viewer -r -l -e .claude
    56→```
    57→
    58→### Exit code
    59→
    60→Exits 0 on success, 1 if any deserialization errors occurred.
    61→
    62→By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
    63→about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
    64→
    65→In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
    66→database to better manage the information, TBD.
    67→
    68→Examples chore file:
    69→```
    70→# Chores-01.md
    71→ 
    72→General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
    73→more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
    74→how and why of a particular chore.
    75→
    76→## Create a binary that lists jj info 
    77→
    78→This binary should list the changeID, commitID, and description title
    79→and using `jj-lib`
    80→```
    81→
    82→## jj tips
    83→
    84→For users new to jj see [jj-tips.md](jj-tips.md).
    85→
    86→```
    87→## Chores format
    88→
    89→Filename: "Chores-XX-.md"
    90→example: chores-01.md
    91→
    92→Format of section labels: "## <short description> (YYYYMMDD X.Y.Z)"
    93→example: "## Topic format description (20260322 0.1.0)"
    94→
    95→Example chore file:
    96→```
    97→# Chores-01.md
    98→ 
    99→General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
   100→more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
   101→how and why of a particular chore.
   102→
   103→## Do something (20260322 1.3.1)
   104→
   105→Describe something
   106→```
   107→
   108→## Versioning during development
   109→
   110→This is using jujustiu, jj + git and we'll see how it goes. Below is my
   111→git workflow, jj will be different but we'll have to discover that as
   112→we go.
   113→
   114→Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based on scope:
   115→
   116→- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump directly to
   117→  `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
   118→- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-devN`, implement
   119→  across multiple commits, final commit removes `-devN`.
   120→
   121→The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before starting.
   122→
   123→For multi-step:
   124→1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
   125→2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
   126→3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
   127→
   128→The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
   129→prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
   130→commits were exploratory vs final.
   131→
   132→## Todo format
   133→
   134→Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
   135→short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
   136→references.
   137→
   138→Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
   139→In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
   140→is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
   141→
   142→Examples:
   143→
   144→# Todo
   145→- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
   146→- Fix bug Y [1]
   147→
   148→# Done
   149→- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
   150→
   151→[1]: chores-01bugs.md#bug-y
   152→
```

id: toolu_01AWWZNFpo5mWrnrMM9axBEG
```
     1→//! Serde types for Claude Code session JSONL files.
     2→//!
     3→//! Each line of a session file is a JSON object with a `"type"` field that
     4→//! determines its shape. The [`Record`] enum uses serde's internally tagged
     5→//! representation (`#[serde(tag = "type")]`) to dispatch to the correct
     6→//! variant struct.
     7→//!
     8→//! # Key patterns
     9→//!
    10→//! **Label strings** — The `RECORD_LABELS` const is the single source of
    11→//! truth for type name strings (e.g. `"assistant"`, `"system"`).
    12→//! [`Record::label()`] indexes into it; [`Record::all_labels()`] returns
    13→//! the full slice. This avoids duplicating strings between the two methods.
    14→//!
    15→//! **Struct field ordering** — Each struct lists required fields first,
    16→//! then `Option` fields below a separator comment. This makes it visually
    17→//! obvious which fields need entries in the `optional_fields()` list.
    18→//!
    19→//! **`deny_unknown_fields`** — Every struct has `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]`
    20→//! so that any JSON key not mapped to a Rust field causes a deserialization
    21→//! error. This ensures we know immediately when Claude Code adds new fields.
    22→//!
    23→//! **`optional_fields()`** — Structs with `Option` fields have a companion
    24→//! `optional_fields()` method listing the camelCase (or snake_case) JSON
    25→//! names of those fields. The `all_optional_fields_seen` test uses these
    26→//! lists to verify every `Option` field is `Some` at least once in test
    27→//! data. Nested fields use dot notation (`"message.usage.speed"`) and
    28→//! array filtering uses brackets (`"message.content[tool_use].caller"`).
    29→//!
    30→//! **Warning**: The `optional_fields()` lists are static — adding an
    31→//! `Option` field to a struct without updating the list means that field
    32→//! silently goes untested. The separator comment in each struct serves as
    33→//! a reminder.
    34→
    35→use serde::Deserialize;
    36→use serde_json::Value;
    37→
    38→/// Top-level record: each line of a Claude Code session JSONL file
    39→/// deserializes into one of these variants, discriminated by the `type` field.
    40→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
    41→#[serde(tag = "type")]
    42→pub enum Record {
    43→    #[serde(rename = "file-history-snapshot")]
    44→    FileHistorySnapshot(FileHistorySnapshotRecord),
    45→
    46→    #[serde(rename = "user")]
    47→    User(Box<UserRecord>),
    48→
    49→    #[serde(rename = "assistant")]
    50→    Assistant(Box<AssistantRecord>),
    51→
    52→    #[serde(rename = "progress")]
    53→    Progress(Box<ProgressRecord>),
    54→
    55→    #[serde(rename = "last-prompt")]
    56→    LastPrompt(LastPromptRecord),
    57→
    58→    #[serde(rename = "queue-operation")]
    59→    QueueOperation(QueueOperationRecord),
    60→
    61→    #[serde(rename = "system")]
    62→    System(Box<SystemRecord>),
    63→
    64→    #[serde(rename = "custom-title")]
    65→    CustomTitle(CustomTitleRecord),
    66→
    67→    #[serde(rename = "agent-name")]
    68→    AgentName(AgentNameRecord),
    69→}
    70→
    71→/// Single source of truth for record type label strings.
    72→const RECORD_LABELS: &[&str] = &[
    73→    "file-history-snapshot",
    74→    "user",
    75→    "assistant",
    76→    "progress",
    77→    "last-prompt",
    78→    "queue-operation",
    79→    "system",
    80→    "custom-title",
    81→    "agent-name",
    82→];
    83→
    84→impl Record {
    85→    /// Returns the type label string for this record variant.
    86→    pub fn label(&self) -> &'static str {
    87→        RECORD_LABELS[match self {
    88→            Record::FileHistorySnapshot(_) => 0,
    89→            Record::User(_) => 1,
    90→            Record::Assistant(_) => 2,
    91→            Record::Progress(_) => 3,
    92→            Record::LastPrompt(_) => 4,
    93→            Record::QueueOperation(_) => 5,
    94→            Record::System(_) => 6,
    95→            Record::CustomTitle(_) => 7,
    96→            Record::AgentName(_) => 8,
    97→        }]
    98→    }
    99→
   100→    /// Returns the full list of known record type labels.
   101→    pub fn all_labels() -> &'static [&'static str] {
   102→        RECORD_LABELS
   103→    }
   104→
   105→    /// Returns the optional fields list for each record type that has them.
   106→    /// Used by tests to verify all Option fields are exercised.
   107→    pub fn optional_fields_by_type() -> Vec<(&'static str, &'static [&'static str])> {
   108→        vec![
   109→            ("user", UserRecord::optional_fields()),
   110→            ("assistant", AssistantRecord::optional_fields()),
   111→            ("progress", ProgressRecord::optional_fields()),
   112→            ("queue-operation", QueueOperationRecord::optional_fields()),
   113→            ("system", SystemRecord::optional_fields()),
   114→        ]
   115→    }
   116→}
   117→

   136→}

   176→            "parentUuid",

   179→            "toolUseResult",
   180→            "isMeta",

   561→#[cfg(test)]
   562→mod tests {
   563→    use super::*;
   564→    use std::collections::HashSet;
   565→    use std::fs::File;
   566→    use std::io::{BufRead, BufReader};
   567→
   568→    const TEST_FILES: &[&str] = &[
   569→        "data/31ba272b-f7c2-436b-a017-269e27a64d07.jsonl",
   570→        "data/997afb98-c6aa-4f4a-92ac-a841e040414b.jsonl",
   571→        "data/092de687-cd0d-4583-b872-bc2908dff3ba.jsonl",
   572→        "data/86fb7a89-abfa-4e84-b862-5983e93c0b3b.jsonl",
   573→        "data/ccs-viewer-tests.jsonl",
   574→        "data/agent-test.jsonl",
   575→    ];
   576→
   577→    fn deserialize_file(path: &str) -> usize {
   578→        deserialize_file_collecting(path, None)
   579→    }
   580→
   581→    fn deserialize_file_collecting(path: &str, mut labels: Option<&mut HashSet<String>>) -> usize {
   582→        let file = File::open(path).expect("sample JSONL file should exist");
   583→        let reader = BufReader::new(file);
   584→        let mut count = 0;
   585→        for (i, line) in reader.lines().enumerate() {
   586→            let line = line.unwrap();
   587→            if line.trim().is_empty() {
   588→                continue;
   589→            }
   590→            let result = serde_json::from_str::<Record>(&line);
   591→            assert!(
   592→                result.is_ok(),
   593→                "line {} failed: {}",
   594→                i + 1,
   595→                result.unwrap_err()
   596→            );
   597→            if let (Ok(record), Some(labels)) = (&result, labels.as_deref_mut()) {
   598→                labels.insert(record.label().to_string());
   599→            }
   600→            count += 1;
   601→        }
   602→        count
   603→    }
   604→
   605→    #[test]
   606→    fn deserialize_sample_31ba() {
   607→        assert_eq!(
   608→            deserialize_file("data/31ba272b-f7c2-436b-a017-269e27a64d07.jsonl"),
   609→            22
   610→        );
   611→    }
   612→
   613→    #[test]
   614→    fn deserialize_sample_997a() {
   615→        assert_eq!(
   616→            deserialize_file("data/997afb98-c6aa-4f4a-92ac-a841e040414b.jsonl"),
   617→            461
   618→        );
   619→    }
   620→
   621→    #[test]
   622→    fn deserialize_sample_092d() {
   623→        assert_eq!(
   624→            deserialize_file("data/092de687-cd0d-4583-b872-bc2908dff3ba.jsonl"),
   625→            224
   626→        );
   627→    }
   628→
   629→    #[test]
   630→    fn deserialize_sample_86fb() {
   631→        assert_eq!(
   632→            deserialize_file("data/86fb7a89-abfa-4e84-b862-5983e93c0b3b.jsonl"),
   633→            301
   634→        );
   635→    }
   636→
   637→    /// Look up a dotted path in a serde_json::Value.
   638→    /// Supports segments like "message", "usage", or array filtering
   639→    /// like "content[tool_use]" (find element in array where type == "tool_use").
   640→    fn value_at_path<'a>(val: &'a Value, path: &str) -> Option<&'a Value> {
   641→        let mut current = val;
   642→        for segment in path.split('.') {
   643→            if let Some(idx) = segment.find('[') {
   644→                let key = &segment[..idx];
   645→                let filter_type = &segment[idx + 1..segment.len() - 1];
   646→                let arr = current.get(key)?.as_array()?;
   647→                current = arr
   648→                    .iter()
   649→                    .find(|el| el.get("type").and_then(|t| t.as_str()) == Some(filter_type))?;
   650→            } else {
   651→                current = current.get(segment)?;
   652→            }
   653→        }
   654→        if current.is_null() {
   655→            None
   656→        } else {
   657→            Some(current)
   658→        }
   659→    }
   660→
   661→    /// Verify every Option field in every struct has at least one non-null
   662→    /// occurrence across test data. This catches Option fields that are defined
   663→    /// in a struct but never actually exercised by any test file.
   664→    ///
   665→    /// WARNING: This test uses a static list of Option fields per record type
   666→    /// (see each struct's `optional_fields()` method). It cannot catch Option
   667→    /// fields that are missing from those lists — those fields will silently
   668→    /// remain untested (always None). When adding a new Option field to a
   669→    /// struct, you must also add its camelCase JSON name to the corresponding
   670→    /// `optional_fields()` list.
   671→    #[test]
   672→    fn all_optional_fields_seen() {
   673→        let field_map = Record::optional_fields_by_type();
   674→        let mut seen: HashSet<(String, String)> = HashSet::new();
   675→
   676→        for path in TEST_FILES {
   677→            let file = File::open(path).expect("test file should exist");
   678→            let reader = BufReader::new(file);
   679→            for line in reader.lines() {
   680→                let line = line.unwrap();
   681→                if line.trim().is_empty() {
   682→                    continue;
   683→                }
   684→                let val: Value = match serde_json::from_str(&line) {
   685→                    Ok(v) => v,
   686→                    Err(_) => continue,
   687→                };
   688→                let record_type = match val.get("type").and_then(|t| t.as_str()) {
   689→                    Some(t) => t,
   690→                    None => continue,
   691→                };
   692→                for &(rtype, fields) in &field_map {
   693→                    if rtype != record_type {
   694→                        continue;
   695→                    }
   696→                    for field in fields {
   697→                        if value_at_path(&val, field).is_some() {
   698→                            seen.insert((record_type.to_string(), field.to_string()));
   699→                        }
   700→                    }
   701→                }
   702→            }
   703→        }
   704→
   705→        let mut never_seen = Vec::new();
   706→        for &(rtype, fields) in &field_map {
   707→            for field in fields {
   708→                if !seen.contains(&(rtype.to_string(), field.to_string())) {
   709→                    never_seen.push(format!("{rtype}.{field}"));
   710→                }
   711→            }
   712→        }
   713→        never_seen.sort();
   714→        assert!(
   715→            never_seen.is_empty(),
   716→            "Option fields never seen as Some in test data: {never_seen:?}"
   717→        );
   718→    }
   719→
   720→    #[test]
   721→    fn deserialize_agent_jsonl() {
   722→        assert_eq!(deserialize_file("data/agent-test.jsonl"), 7);
   723→    }
   724→
   725→    #[test]
   726→    fn deserialize_agent_meta() {
   727→        let path = "data/agent-adf742daa2c66fe48.meta.json";
   728→        let file = File::open(path).expect("agent meta file should exist");
   729→        let meta: AgentMeta = serde_json::from_reader(file).expect("agent meta should deserialize");
   730→        assert_eq!(meta.agent_type, "Explore");
   731→        assert!(meta.description.is_some());
   732→    }
   733→
   734→    #[test]
   735→    fn deserialize_agent_meta_no_description() {
   736→        let path = "data/agent-ada8e90bba5770efc.meta.json";
   737→        let file = File::open(path).expect("agent meta file should exist");
   738→        let meta: AgentMeta = serde_json::from_reader(file).expect("agent meta should deserialize");
   739→        assert_eq!(meta.agent_type, "Explore");
   740→        assert!(meta.description.is_none());
   741→    }
   742→
   743→    #[test]
   744→    fn all_variants_covered() {
   745→        let mut seen = HashSet::new();
   746→        for path in TEST_FILES {
   747→            deserialize_file_collecting(path, Some(&mut seen));
   748→        }
   749→        let expected: HashSet<String> =
   750→            Record::all_labels().iter().map(|s| s.to_string()).collect();
   751→        let missing: Vec<_> = expected.difference(&seen).collect();
   752→        assert!(
   753→            missing.is_empty(),
   754→            "Record variants not covered by test data: {missing:?}"
   755→        );
   756→    }
   757→}
   758→
```

Here's my analysis of the 12 error categories:

**Simple field additions** (add as `Option` to existing structs):
1. **2963x** `thinkingMetadata` → add to `UserRecord`
2. **26x** `isVisibleInTranscriptOnly` → add to `UserRecord`
3. **2106x** `data_mtime` → add to `AgentMeta`
4. **9x** `id` → add to `AgentMeta`
5. **2x** `taskDescription` → add to `ProgressData`
6. **1x** `error` → add to `SystemRecord`

**Type fixes** (field exists but wrong type):
7. **1475x** null string in `assistant` — a required `String` field is sometimes null; need to find which one and make it `Option<String>`
8. **49x** null string in `system` — same issue
9. **42x** sequence instead of string in `queue-operation` — `content` is sometimes an array; change `Option<String>` → `Option<Value>`

**New variant:**
10. **689x** `summary` — add a `Summary` variant to the `Record` enum

**Unfixable / out of scope:**
11. **5x** `line2.jsonl` missing `type` — not Claude Code session files
12. **4x** malformed JSON at column 1 — corrupt data

So 10 code fixes, 2 data issues we can't fix. This is mechanical — I'd recommend **single-step** to a new version (0.12.0). 

Before starting, I need to find sample data for the null-string errors (#7, #8) to identify which fields are null. Want me to proceed?

Then a final 0.12.0 commit to remove `-devN`. Additional `-devX` as needed if we discover issues along the way.

3. The user record with `isVisibleInTranscriptOnly` also has `isCompactSummary` — another unknown field we need to add.

    10→#[derive(Parser)]
    11→#[command(version, about = "Claude Code session JSONL viewer")]
    12→struct Cli {
    13→    /// File or directory glob patterns to process
    14→    #[arg(required = true)]
    15→    patterns: Vec<String>,
    16→
    17→    /// Show per-file summary lines
    18→    #[arg(short, long)]

    20→
    21→    /// Show grouped error details
    22→    #[arg(short, long)]
    23→    errors: bool,
    24→
    25→    /// Recursively search directories for matching files
    26→    #[arg(short, long)]
    27→    recursive: bool,
    28→
    29→    /// File glob pattern for recursive mode (repeatable, default: *.jsonl)
    30→    #[arg(long = "glob")]
    31→    globs: Vec<String>,
    32→}
    33→
    34→/// Resolve CLI patterns into a list of file paths.
    35→fn resolve_files(cli: &Cli) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    36→    let mut files = Vec::new();
    37→
    38→    if cli.recursive {
    39→        let file_globs: Vec<&str> = if cli.globs.is_empty() {
    40→            vec!["*.jsonl", "*.meta.json"]
    41→        } else {
    42→            cli.globs.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()
    43→        };
    44→
    45→        // Expand positional patterns as directory globs.
    46→        for pattern in &cli.patterns {
    47→            let dirs: Vec<PathBuf> = match glob::glob(pattern) {
    48→                Ok(paths) => paths
    49→                    .filter_map(|p| p.ok())
    50→                    .filter(|p| p.is_dir())
    51→                    .collect(),
    52→                Err(e) => {
    53→                    eprintln!("Bad pattern {pattern:?}: {e}");
    54→                    std::process::exit(1);
    55→                }
    56→            };
    57→            if dirs.is_empty() {
    58→                eprintln!("No directories match {pattern:?}");
    59→                std::process::exit(1);
    60→            }
    61→            for dir in &dirs {
    62→                for fg in &file_globs {
    63→                    let full = format!("{}/**/{fg}", dir.display());
    64→                    match glob::glob(&full) {
    65→                        Ok(paths) => {
    66→                            for entry in paths.flatten() {
    67→                                if entry.is_file() {
    68→                                    files.push(entry);
    69→                                }
    70→                            }
    71→                        }
    72→                        Err(e) => {
    73→                            eprintln!("Bad glob {full:?}: {e}");
    74→                            std::process::exit(1);
    75→                        }
    76→                    }
    77→                }
    78→            }
    79→        }
    80→    } else {
    81→        let file_globs: Vec<&str> = if cli.globs.is_empty() {
    82→            vec!["*.jsonl", "*.meta.json"]
    83→        } else {
    84→            cli.globs.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()
    85→        };
    86→
    87→        for pattern in &cli.patterns {
    88→            // Check if pattern is a literal directory path first.
    89→            let path = PathBuf::from(pattern);
    90→            if path.is_dir() {
    91→                // Directory: expand file globs inside it (non-recursive).
    92→                for fg in &file_globs {
    93→                    let full = format!("{}/{fg}", path.display());
    94→                    match glob::glob(&full) {
    95→                        Ok(paths) => {
    96→                            for entry in paths.flatten() {
    97→                                if entry.is_file() {
    98→                                    files.push(entry);
    99→                                }
   100→                            }
   101→                        }
   102→                        Err(e) => {
   103→                            eprintln!("Bad glob {full:?}: {e}");
   104→                            std::process::exit(1);
   105→                        }
   106→                    }
   107→                }
   108→            } else {
   109→                // Treat as file glob pattern.
   110→                match glob::glob(pattern) {
   111→                    Ok(paths) => {
   112→                        let mut matched = Vec::new();
   113→                        for entry in paths.flatten() {
   114→                            if entry.is_file() {
   115→                                matched.push(entry);
   116→                            } else if entry.is_dir() {
   117→                                // Glob matched a directory: expand file
   118→                                // globs inside it (non-recursive).
   119→                                for fg in &file_globs {
   120→                                    let full = format!("{}/{fg}", entry.display());
   121→                                    if let Ok(inner) = glob::glob(&full) {
   122→                                        for f in inner.flatten() {

   125→                                            }
   126→                                        }
   127→                                    }
   128→                                }
   129→                            }
   130→                        }

   134→                        }

   138→                        eprintln!("Bad pattern {pattern:?}: {e}");
   139→                        std::process::exit(1);
   140→                    }
   141→                }
   142→            }
   143→        }
   144→    }
   145→
   146→    files.sort();
   147→    files.dedup();
   148→    files
   149→}
   150→
   151→/// Key for grouping errors: the serde error message + record type.
   152→#[derive(Hash, Eq, PartialEq)]
   153→struct ErrorKey {
   154→    message: String,
   155→    record_type: String,
   156→}
   157→
   158→/// Tracks one group of identical errors across files.
   159→struct ErrorGroup {
   160→    count: usize,

   163→    example_line: usize,
   164→    /// How many distinct files contain this error.
   165→    file_count: usize,
   166→    /// Track which files we've seen (by index) to count distinct files.

   168→}

   170→fn main() {

   172→    let files = resolve_files(&cli);
   173→
   174→    if files.is_empty() {

   177→    }

   188→
   189→        let filename = path

   200→                    if cli.list {
   201→                        println!("{filename}: agent-meta (ok)");

   213→                        example_line: 1,
   214→                        file_count: 0,
   215→                        seen_files: Vec::new(),
   216→                    });

   224→                    }
   225→                }
   226→            }
   227→            continue;
   228→        }
   229→
   230→        let reader = BufReader::new(file);

The glob picks up `*.meta.json` which catches mypy cache files. The 2106 + 9 errors are **not agent meta files at all**. Two options:

2. Add the mypy fields to `AgentMeta` (wrong — they're different formats)

I'll go with option 1. Let me also revert the `data_mtime`/`id` additions to `AgentMeta` since those were for mypy files.

> DEVELOPER

I wanted to verify you changed default `*.meta.json` glob and you did.

But I wonder if we should tighten up `*.jsonl`  All UUID(?).jsonl I took a quick look at started with '{"type":...' and the agent-*.jsonl file seemed to start with '{"parentUuid":'. So maybe if the first line of a .jsonl file doesn't start with those two keys we skip it, but record the file path and output skipped files as another flag. Thoughts.

> AGENT

Good idea. A first-line sniff test is more robust than filename patterns — it catches non-CCS `.jsonl` files regardless of naming convention. And it's cheap (read one line, check two prefixes).

I'd suggest:

- Read the first line of each `.jsonl` file
- If it starts with `{"type":` or `{"parentUuid":` → process normally
- Otherwise → skip, count it, optionally report it
- New flag: `--skipped` / `-s` to list skipped files
- Always include skipped count in the summary line (like errors)

This would eliminate the `line2.jsonl` and `logs.jsonl`-type false positives without needing filename heuristics.

Should I add this to dev1, or make it a separate dev step?

> DEVELOPER

Separate, let's call it -dev1.1 so our major steps don't change

> AGENT

Makes sense. Let me finish dev1 first (the field additions), then we'll do dev1.1 for the sniff test.

> DEVELOPER

You didn't comment on my changing exit code processing

> AGENT

Good catch — I missed that. The exit code 1 from `ccs-viewer` is expected behavior (it exits 1 when there are errors), not a failure. The `cargo install` exit code was also 0 — the shell just reported the combined exit code from the `&&` chain.

So nothing actually went wrong there. Did you want me to change the exit code behavior, or were you just flagging that I didn't acknowledge it?

> DEVELOPER

I think we should be able to override at least, thoughts?

> AGENT

Agreed. A `--strict` / `--no-strict` flag makes sense:

- Default (current): exit 1 if any deserialization errors
- `--no-strict`: always exit 0 (useful for pipelines, exploratory scanning)

Or flip it — default to exit 0 and `--strict` opts into exit 1. That's friendlier for the common "scan and see what's broken" use case.

My preference would be to keep the current default (exit 1 on errors) since it's the safer default for scripts, and add `--no-strict` to suppress it. But I could see the argument either way.

Which direction do you prefer? And should this go into dev1.1 alongside the sniff test, or its own step?

> DEVELOPER

default to to --no-strict makes sense I as a user look that the output and don't care about the exit code, we might want to tighten the error codes and have a particular exit code when errors is non-zero, that will make it easier for a script to id bad shit happened compared to "errors".

> AGENT

Makes sense. How about:

- Exit 0: success (default, even with deser errors)
- Exit 1: actual failures (can't open file, bad args, no files match)
- Exit 2: deserialization errors present (opt-in via `--strict`)

That way scripts can distinguish "tool broke" from "data has issues." And the default is user-friendly — you just look at the output.

Bundle this with dev1.1 since it's a small CLI behavior change? Or separate?

> DEVELOPER

SG, let's do that in dev1.1

    22→    #[arg(short, long)]
    23→    errors: bool,
    24→
    25→    /// Recursively search directories for matching files
    26→    #[arg(short, long)]
    27→    recursive: bool,
    28→
    29→    /// File glob pattern for recursive mode (repeatable, default: *.jsonl)
    30→    #[arg(long = "glob")]
    31→    globs: Vec<String>,
    32→}
    33→
    34→/// Resolve CLI patterns into a list of file paths.
    35→fn resolve_files(cli: &Cli) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    36→    let mut files = Vec::new();
    37→
    38→    if cli.recursive {
    39→        let file_globs: Vec<&str> = if cli.globs.is_empty() {
    40→            vec!["*.jsonl", "agent-*.meta.json"]
    41→        } else {
    42→            cli.globs.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()
    43→        };
    44→
    45→        // Expand positional patterns as directory globs.
    46→        for pattern in &cli.patterns {
    47→            let dirs: Vec<PathBuf> = match glob::glob(pattern) {
    48→                Ok(paths) => paths
    49→                    .filter_map(|p| p.ok())
    50→                    .filter(|p| p.is_dir())
    51→                    .collect(),
    52→                Err(e) => {
    53→                    eprintln!("Bad pattern {pattern:?}: {e}");
    54→                    std::process::exit(1);
    55→                }
    56→            };
    57→            if dirs.is_empty() {
    58→                eprintln!("No directories match {pattern:?}");
    59→                std::process::exit(1);
    60→            }
    61→            for dir in &dirs {
    62→                for fg in &file_globs {
    63→                    let full = format!("{}/**/{fg}", dir.display());
    64→                    match glob::glob(&full) {
    65→                        Ok(paths) => {
    66→                            for entry in paths.flatten() {
    67→                                if entry.is_file() {
    68→                                    files.push(entry);
    69→                                }
    70→                            }
    71→                        }
    72→                        Err(e) => {
    73→                            eprintln!("Bad glob {full:?}: {e}");
    74→                            std::process::exit(1);
    75→                        }
    76→                    }
    77→                }
    78→            }
    79→        }
    80→    } else {
    81→        let file_globs: Vec<&str> = if cli.globs.is_empty() {
    82→            vec!["*.jsonl", "agent-*.meta.json"]
    83→        } else {
    84→            cli.globs.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()
    85→        };
    86→
    87→        for pattern in &cli.patterns {
    88→            // Check if pattern is a literal directory path first.
    89→            let path = PathBuf::from(pattern);
    90→            if path.is_dir() {
    91→                // Directory: expand file globs inside it (non-recursive).
    92→                for fg in &file_globs {
    93→                    let full = format!("{}/{fg}", path.display());
    94→                    match glob::glob(&full) {
    95→                        Ok(paths) => {
    96→                            for entry in paths.flatten() {
    97→                                if entry.is_file() {
    98→                                    files.push(entry);
    99→                                }
   100→                            }
   101→                        }
   102→                        Err(e) => {
   103→                            eprintln!("Bad glob {full:?}: {e}");
   104→                            std::process::exit(1);
   105→                        }
   106→                    }
   107→                }
   108→            } else {
   109→                // Treat as file glob pattern.
   110→                match glob::glob(pattern) {
   111→                    Ok(paths) => {
   112→                        let mut matched = Vec::new();
   113→                        for entry in paths.flatten() {
   114→                            if entry.is_file() {
   115→                                matched.push(entry);
   116→                            } else if entry.is_dir() {
   117→                                // Glob matched a directory: expand file
   118→                                // globs inside it (non-recursive).
   119→                                for fg in &file_globs {
   120→                                    let full = format!("{}/{fg}", entry.display());
   121→                                    if let Ok(inner) = glob::glob(&full) {
   122→                                        for f in inner.flatten() {
   123→                                            if f.is_file() {
   124→                                                matched.push(f);
   125→                                            }
   126→                                        }
   127→                                    }
   128→                                }
   129→                            }
   130→                        }
   131→                        if matched.is_empty() {
   132→                            eprintln!("No files match {pattern:?}");
   133→                            std::process::exit(1);
   134→                        }
   135→                        files.extend(matched);
   136→                    }
   137→                    Err(e) => {
   138→                        eprintln!("Bad pattern {pattern:?}: {e}");
   139→                        std::process::exit(1);
   140→                    }
   141→                }
   142→            }
   143→        }
   144→    }
   145→
   146→    files.sort();
   147→    files.dedup();
   148→    files
   149→}
   150→
   151→/// Key for grouping errors: the serde error message + record type.
   152→#[derive(Hash, Eq, PartialEq)]
   153→struct ErrorKey {
   154→    message: String,
   155→    record_type: String,
   156→}
   157→
   158→/// Tracks one group of identical errors across files.
   159→struct ErrorGroup {
   160→    count: usize,
   161→    /// One representative file:line for grabbing test data.
   162→    example_file: String,
   163→    example_line: usize,
   164→    /// How many distinct files contain this error.
   165→    file_count: usize,
   166→    /// Track which files we've seen (by index) to count distinct files.
   167→    seen_files: Vec<usize>,
   168→}
   169→
   170→fn main() {
   171→    let cli = Cli::parse();
   172→    let files = resolve_files(&cli);
   173→
   174→    if files.is_empty() {
   175→        eprintln!("No files to process");
   176→        std::process::exit(1);
   177→    }
   178→
   179→    let mut total_records: usize = 0;
   180→    let mut total_errors: usize = 0;
   181→    let mut error_groups: HashMap<ErrorKey, ErrorGroup> = HashMap::new();
   182→
   183→    for (file_idx, path) in files.iter().enumerate() {
   184→        let file = File::open(path).unwrap_or_else(|e| {
   185→            eprintln!("Error opening {}: {e}", path.display());
   186→            std::process::exit(1);
   187→        });
   188→
   189→        let filename = path
   190→            .file_name()
   191→            .map(|f| f.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
   192→            .unwrap_or_else(|| path.display().to_string());
   193→
   194→        // Handle .meta.json files as single-object JSON (not JSONL).
   195→        if filename.ends_with(".meta.json") {
   196→            let result: Result<AgentMeta, _> = serde_json::from_reader(file);
   197→            match result {
   198→                Ok(_meta) => {
   199→                    total_records += 1;
   200→                    if cli.list {
   201→                        println!("{filename}: agent-meta (ok)");
   202→                    }
   203→                }
   204→                Err(e) => {
   205→                    total_errors += 1;
   206→                    let key = ErrorKey {
   207→                        message: format!("{e}"),
   208→                        record_type: "agent-meta".to_string(),
   209→                    };
   210→                    let group = error_groups.entry(key).or_insert_with(|| ErrorGroup {
   211→                        count: 0,
   212→                        example_file: filename.clone(),
   213→                        example_line: 1,
   214→                        file_count: 0,
   215→                        seen_files: Vec::new(),
   216→                    });
   217→                    group.count += 1;
   218→                    if !group.seen_files.contains(&file_idx) {
   219→                        group.seen_files.push(file_idx);
   220→                        group.file_count += 1;
   221→                    }
   222→                    if cli.list {
   223→                        println!("{filename}: agent-meta (error)");
   224→                    }
   225→                }
   226→            }
   227→            continue;
   228→        }
   229→
   230→        let reader = BufReader::new(file);
   231→        let mut counts: BTreeMap<&str, usize> = BTreeMap::new();
   232→        let mut file_errors: usize = 0;
   233→
   234→        for (i, line) in reader.lines().enumerate() {
   235→            let line = line.unwrap_or_else(|e| {
   236→                eprintln!("Error reading line {}: {e}", i + 1);
   237→                std::process::exit(1);
   238→            });
   239→            if line.trim().is_empty() {
   240→                continue;
   241→            }
   242→            match serde_json::from_str::<Record>(&line) {
   243→                Ok(record) => {
   244→                    *counts.entry(record.label()).or_insert(0) += 1;
   245→                }
   246→                Err(e) => {
   247→                    file_errors += 1;
   248→                    let record_type = serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(&line)
   249→                        .ok()
   250→                        .and_then(|v| v.get("type")?.as_str().map(String::from))
   251→                        .unwrap_or_else(|| "unknown".to_string());
   252→
   253→                    let key = ErrorKey {
   254→                        message: format!("{e}"),
   255→                        record_type,
   256→                    };
   257→                    let group = error_groups.entry(key).or_insert_with(|| ErrorGroup {
   258→                        count: 0,
   259→                        example_file: filename.clone(),
   260→                        example_line: i + 1,
   261→                        file_count: 0,
   262→                        seen_files: Vec::new(),
   263→                    });
   264→                    group.count += 1;
   265→                    if !group.seen_files.contains(&file_idx) {
   266→                        group.seen_files.push(file_idx);
   267→                        group.file_count += 1;
   268→                    }
   269→                }
   270→            }
   271→        }
   272→
   273→        let file_total: usize = counts.values().sum();
   274→        total_records += file_total;
   275→        total_errors += file_errors;
   276→
   277→        if cli.list {
   278→            let mut parts = vec![
   279→                format!("errors: {file_errors}"),
   280→                format!("records: {file_total}"),
   281→            ];
   282→            for (label, count) in &counts {
   283→                parts.push(format!("{label}: {count}"));
   284→            }
   285→            println!("{filename}: {}", parts.join(", "));
   286→        }
   287→    }
   288→
   289→    let file_count = files.len();
   290→    println!(
   291→        "{}Summary: {file_count} file(s), {total_records} records, {total_errors} errors",
   292→        if cli.list { "\n" } else { "" }
   293→    );
   294→
   295→    if cli.errors && !error_groups.is_empty() {
   296→        println!("\nErrors:");
   297→        let mut groups: Vec<_> = error_groups.into_iter().collect();
   298→        groups.sort_by(|a, b| b.1.count.cmp(&a.1.count));
   299→        for (key, group) in &groups {
   300→            println!(
   301→                "  {}x {} in {} ({}:{} in {} file(s))",
   302→                group.count,
   303→                key.message,
   304→                key.record_type,
   305→                group.example_file,
   306→                group.example_line,
   307→                group.file_count,
   308→            );
   309→        }
   310→    }
   311→
   312→    if total_errors > 0 {
   313→        std::process::exit(1);
   314→    }
   315→}
   316→
```

> AGENT

`--help` already exits 0 — that's clap's default behavior. Same for `--version`. So the exit code semantics are clean:

- 0: success, `--help`, `--version`
- 1: tool failure
- 2: deser errors with `--strict`

     1→# ccs viewer by cc
     2→
     3→This is claude-code viewer which claude-code is writing.
     4→in hwr.claude/ is a json file where I asked code code to
     5→create a hello world in rust. We are going to implement
     6→an app at displays that conversation.
     7→
     8→This is the main repo of a dual-repo convention for using
     9→a bot to help in the development of a coding project. The goal
    10→is that this main repo contains the "what", while the partner
    11→bot repo contains "why" and "how". The key to the convention
    12→is each change is cross-referenced to the other. Thus there
    13→is a coherent story of the development of the project across time.
    14→
    15→The beginnings of that tool is [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1)
    16→which currently does achieve this goal, but is being used as a
    17→first test bed.
    18→
    19→## jj Tips for Git Users
    20→
    21→See [Steve Klabnik](https://github.com/steveklabnik)
    22→[Jujutsu-tutorial](https://steveklabnik.github.io/jujutsu-tutorial)
    23→and [jj docs](https://docs.jj-vcs.dev/latest/).
    24→
    25→### Initial Commit for a repo
    26→
    27→Create create directory add files.
    28→
    29→Minimal commands to push 
    30→
    31→```
    32→jj git init .
    33→jj describe
    34→jj git remote add origin git@github.com:winksaville/vc-template-x1
    35→jj bookmark create main -r @
    36→jj bookmark track main --remote=origin
    37→jj git push
    38→```
    39→
    40→### Push a change to main
    41→
    42→Assuming that this is to be push to main you
    43→set the bookmark to the appropriate commit and
    44→then just push:
    45→
    46→```
    47→jj bookmark set main -r @
    48→jj git push
    49→```

   174→
   175→### Why `jj log` shows fewer commits than `gitk`
   176→
   177→If you're coming from git, jj's log output can be surprising compared to
   178→tools like `gitk --all`.
   179→
   180→jj tracks *changes* (identified by change IDs), not individual git commits.
   181→When you rewrite a change (`jj describe`, `jj rebase`, `jj squash`, etc.),
   182→jj creates a new git commit and keeps the old one under `refs/jj/keep/*` as
   183→undo history. `gitk --all` sees all of these obsolete commits; `jj log` only
   184→shows the current version of each change.
   185→
   186→### Useful commands
   187→
   188→| Command | Description |
   189→|---------|-------------|
   190→| `jj log` | Show recent visible commits (default revset) |
   191→| `jj log -r ::@` | Show **all** ancestors of the working copy |
   192→| `jj log -r 'all()'` | Show all non-hidden commits (needed if you have multiple heads/branches) |
   193→| `jj st | Show the status of the Working and Parent commits |
   194→| `jj st -r <chid> | Status of the commit, <chid> such as `@`, `@-`, `xyz` |
   195→| `jj show | Show the Working commit, -r @ |
   196→| `jj show -r <chid> | Show the commit, <chid> such as `@`, `@-`, `xyz` |
   197→| `jj evolog -r <chid>` | Show the evolution history of a single change |
   198→| `jj op log` | Show operation history (each rewrite operation) |
   199→
   200→
   201→In a single-branch workflow, `jj log -r ::@` and `jj log -r 'all()'` give
   202→the same result. Use `all()` when you have multiple branches or heads.
   203→
   204→## Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers
   205→
   206→Commits in each repo use [git trailers](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-interpret-trailers)
   207→to cross-reference their counterpart in the other repo. The `ochid`
   208→(Other Change ID) trailer contains a workspace-root-relative path
   209→and jj changeID:
   210→
   211→```
   212→ochid: /.claude/xvzvruqo   # points to a .claude repo change
   213→ochid: /wtpmottv            # points to an app repo change
   214→```
   215→
   216→Paths always start with `/` (the workspace root, i.e. vc-x1).
   217→Each repo has a `.vc-config.toml` that identifies its location
   218→within the workspace, so tools can resolve these paths locally.
   219→
   220→For full details see:
   221→- [Git trailer convention](./notes/chores-01.md#git-trailer-convention)
   222→  — [ochid (Other Change ID)](./notes/chores-01.md#ochid-other-change-id)
   223→  — [ChangeID path syntax](./notes/chores-01.md#changeid-path-syntax)
   224→  — [.vc-config.toml](./notes/chores-01.md#vc-configtoml)
   225→

   240→

```
     1→# Notes
     2→
     3→This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
     4→Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
     5→
     6→## CLI Usage
     7→
     8→```
     9→ccs-viewer [OPTIONS] <PATTERNS>...
    10→```
    11→
    12→By default, only the summary line is printed. Use flags for more detail.
    13→
    14→### Options
    15→
    16→| Flag | Short | Description |
    17→|------|-------|-------------|
    18→| `--list` | `-l` | Show per-file summary lines |
    19→| `--errors` | `-e` | Show grouped error details after summary |
    20→| `--recursive` | `-r` | Treat positional args as directories, search recursively |
    21→| `--glob <PAT>` | | File pattern for recursive mode (repeatable, default: `*.jsonl`, `agent-*.meta.json`) |
    22→| `--strict` | | Exit 2 if deserialization errors are present |
    23→| `--skipped` | `-s` | Show files skipped by the first-line sniff test |
    24→| `--version` | `-V` | Print version |
    25→| `--help` | `-h` | Print help |
    26→
    27→### Positional arguments
    28→
    29→Without `-r`: file glob patterns expanded by the program.
    30→With `-r`: directory paths (or directory globs) to search recursively.
    31→
    32→### Examples
    33→
    34→```
    35→# Summary only (default)
    36→ccs-viewer "data/*.jsonl"
    37→
    38→# Per-file list + summary
    39→ccs-viewer -l "data/*.jsonl"
    40→
    41→# Summary + error details
    42→ccs-viewer -e "data/*.jsonl"
    43→
    44→# Recursive search in a directory
    45→ccs-viewer -r .claude
    46→
    47→# Recursive with custom file patterns
    48→ccs-viewer -r --glob "*.jsonl" --glob "*.json" .claude
    49→
    50→# Multiple directories
    51→ccs-viewer -r .claude ../vc-x1/.claude
    52→
    53→# Directory glob
    54→ccs-viewer -r "../*/.claude"
    55→
    56→# All flags
    57→ccs-viewer -r -l -e .claude
    58→```
    59→
    60→### Exit code
    61→
    62→- 0: success (default, even with deserialization errors)
    63→- 1: tool failure (bad args, can't open file, no files match)
    64→- 2: deserialization errors present (only with `--strict`)
    65→
    66→By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
    67→about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
    68→
    69→In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
    70→database to better manage the information, TBD.
    71→
    72→Examples chore file:
    73→```
    74→# Chores-01.md
    75→ 
    76→General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
    77→more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
    78→how and why of a particular chore.
    79→
    80→## Create a binary that lists jj info 
    81→
    82→This binary should list the changeID, commitID, and description title
    83→and using `jj-lib`
    84→```
    85→
    86→## jj tips
    87→
    88→For users new to jj see [jj-tips.md](jj-tips.md).
    89→
    90→```
    91→## Chores format
    92→
    93→Filename: "Chores-XX-.md"
    94→example: chores-01.md
    95→
    96→Format of section labels: "## <short description> (YYYYMMDD X.Y.Z)"
    97→example: "## Topic format description (20260322 0.1.0)"
    98→
    99→Example chore file:
   100→```
   101→# Chores-01.md
   102→ 
   103→General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
   104→more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
   105→how and why of a particular chore.
   106→
   107→## Do something (20260322 1.3.1)
   108→
   109→Describe something
   110→```
   111→
   112→## Versioning during development
   113→
   114→This is using jujustiu, jj + git and we'll see how it goes. Below is my
   115→git workflow, jj will be different but we'll have to discover that as
   116→we go.
   117→
   118→Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based on scope:
   119→
   120→- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump directly to
   121→  `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
   122→- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-devN`, implement
   123→  across multiple commits, final commit removes `-devN`.
   124→
   125→The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before starting.
   126→
   127→For multi-step:
   128→1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
   129→2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
   130→3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
   131→
   132→The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
   133→prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
   134→commits were exploratory vs final.
   135→
   136→## Todo format
   137→
   138→Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
   139→short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
   140→references.
   141→
   142→Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
   143→In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
   144→is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
   145→
   146→Examples:
   147→
   148→# Todo
   149→- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
   150→- Fix bug Y [1]
   151→
   152→# Done
   153→- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
   154→
   155→[1]: chores-01bugs.md#bug-y
   156→
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/README.md",
  "old_string": "# ccs viewer by cc\n\nThis is claude-code viewer which claude-code is writing.\nin hwr.claude/ is a json file where I asked code code to\ncreate a hello world in rust. We are going to implement\nan app at displays that conversation.\n\nThis is the main repo of a dual-repo convention for using\na bot to help in the development of a coding project. The goal\nis that this main repo contains the \"what\", while the partner\nbot repo contains \"why\" and \"how\". The key to the convention\nis each change is cross-referenced to the other. Thus there\nis a coherent story of the development of the project across time.\n\nThe beginnings of that tool is [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1)\nwhich currently does achieve this goal, but is being used as a\nfirst test bed.\n\n## jj Tips for Git Users",
  "new_string": "# ccs-viewer\n\nA CLI tool for viewing and validating Claude Code session files\n(JSONL and agent meta.json). Written in Rust, built by Claude Code.\n\nThis is the main repo of a dual-repo convention for using\na bot to help in the development of a coding project. The goal\nis that this main repo contains the \"what\", while the partner\nbot repo contains \"why\" and \"how\". The key to the convention\nis each change is cross-referenced to the other. Thus there\nis a coherent story of the development of the project across time.\n\nThe beginnings of that tool is [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1)\nwhich currently does achieve this goal, but is being used as a\nfirst test bed.\n\n## CLI Usage\n\n```\nccs-viewer [OPTIONS] <PATTERNS>...\n```\n\nBy default, only the summary line is printed. Use flags for more detail.\n\n### Options\n\n| Flag | Short | Description |\n|------|-------|-------------|\n| `--list` | `-l` | Show per-file summary lines |\n| `--errors` | `-e` | Show grouped error details |\n| `--recursive` | `-r` | Treat positional args as directories, search recursively |\n| `--glob <PAT>` | | File pattern for recursive mode (repeatable, default: `*.jsonl`, `agent-*.meta.json`) |\n| `--strict` | | Exit 2 if deserialization errors are present |\n| `--skipped` | `-s` | Show files skipped by the first-line sniff test |\n| `--version` | `-V` | Print version |\n| `--help` | `-h` | Print help |\n\n### Positional arguments\n\nWithout `-r`: file glob patterns expanded by the program.\nWith `-r`: directory paths (or directory globs) to search recursively.\n\n### Examples\n\n```bash\n# Summary only (default)\nccs-viewer \"data/*.jsonl\"\n\n# Per-file list + summary\nccs-viewer -l \"data/*.jsonl\"\n\n# Summary + error details\nccs-viewer -e \"data/*.jsonl\"\n\n# Recursive search in a directory\nccs-viewer -r .claude\n\n# Recursive with custom file patterns\nccs-viewer -r --glob \"*.jsonl\" --glob \"*.json\" .claude\n\n# Multiple directories\nccs-viewer -r .claude ../vc-x1/.claude\n\n# Directory glob\nccs-viewer -r \"../*/.claude\"\n\n# All detail flags\nccs-viewer -r -l -e -s .claude\n\n# Strict mode for CI\nccs-viewer -r --strict .claude\n```\n\n### Example output\n\n```\n$ ccs-viewer -e -r .claude\nErrors:\n  2x unknown variant `summary` at line 1 column 17 in summary (abc.jsonl:1 in 2 file(s))\n  1x invalid type: null, expected a string in assistant (def.jsonl:1 in 1 file(s))\n\nSummary: 42 file(s), 1523 records, 3 errors, 2 skipped\n```\n\nOutput order: per-file list (if `-l`) → errors (if `-e`) → skipped\nfiles (if `-s`) → **summary** (always last).\n\n### Exit codes\n\n- 0: success (default, even with deserialization errors)\n- 1: tool failure (bad args, can't open file, no files match)\n- 2: deserialization errors present (only with `--strict`)\n\n### First-line sniff test\n\nJSONL files are checked before processing: if the first line doesn't\nstart with `{\"type\":` or `{\"parentUuid\":`, the file is skipped. This\nfilters out non-Claude-Code JSONL files (benchmark logs, etc.) without\nrelying on filename patterns. Use `-s` to see which files were skipped.\n\n## jj Tips for Git Users"
}
```

    56→
    57→## jj Basics
    58→
    59→- `jj st -R .` / `jj st -R .claude` — show working copy status
    60→- `jj log -R .` / `jj log -R .claude` — show commit log
    61→- `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — finalize working copy into a commit
    62→- `jj describe -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — set description without committing
    63→- `jj git push --bookmark <name> -R <repo>` — push a bookmark (no
    64→  `--allow-new` flag; jj pushes new bookmarks without special flags)
    65→- In jj, the working copy (@) is always a mutable commit being edited.
    66→  `jj commit` finalizes it and creates a new empty working copy on top.
    67→- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active
    68→  session because session data updates continuously.
    69→

    70→## Commit Message Style
    71→
    72→Use [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with
    73→a version suffix:
    74→
    75→```
    76→<type>: <short description> (<version>)

    78→
    79→- **Title**: max 52 chars, short summary of *what* changed.
    80→  Include the version. Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`,
    81→  `test`, `docs`, `chore`.
    82→- **Body**: max 72 chars per line. Start with a short explanatory
    83→  sentence or paragraph, then a blank line, then a bulleted list
    84→  of changes. Keep items as readable sentences.
    85→- Examples:
    86→  - `feat: add fix-ochid subcommand (0.22.0)`
    87→  - `fix: fix-ochid prefix bug (0.22.1)`

id: toolu_01NHRMZgNZp3R3CnYGzetdaw
```
Here are the findings from each sample file:

---

**1. Assistant record with null String field** (`agent-001a8ba8.jsonl`, line 1):

The JSON has `"parentUuid":null` at the top level. In `AssistantRecord` (line 249 of `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/types.rs`), `parent_uuid` is declared as `pub parent_uuid: String` -- a required, non-optional String. When the JSON contains `null`, serde fails with "invalid type: null, expected a string".

Also notable: `"stop_reason":null` and `"stop_sequence":null` appear, but those are already `Option<String>` so they're fine.

**Fields to fix in `AssistantRecord`**: `parent_uuid: String` should become `parent_uuid: Option<String>`.

---

**2. System record with null String field** (`4cbfe9ba-5623-4017-bc3e-31e1fbeedf78.jsonl`, line 2):

The JSON has `"parentUuid":null` at the top level. In `SystemRecord` (line 498), `parent_uuid` is declared as `pub parent_uuid: String` -- a required, non-optional String.

**Fields to fix in `SystemRecord`**: `parent_uuid: String` should become `parent_uuid: Option<String>`.

---

**3. Queue-operation record with content as sequence** (`e7a3a84e-1fd1-46c9-a2ec-276704f45cf6.jsonl`, line 1):

The JSON has `"content":[{"type":"text","text":"flesh out equilateral_tri_hole..."}]` -- an array of content blocks. In `QueueOperationRecord` (line 480), `content` is declared as `pub content: Option<String>`. It can handle `null` and plain strings, but not an array/sequence.

**Fields to fix in `QueueOperationRecord`**: `content: Option<String>` needs to accept either a string or an array. This requires an enum type (similar to `UserContent` or `ToolResultContent`) or changing to `Option<Value>`.

---

**Summary of required type changes in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/types.rs`:**

| Struct | Field | Current Type | Needed Type | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AssistantRecord` (line 249) | `parent_uuid` | `String` | `Option<String>` | Can be `null` |
| `SystemRecord` (line 498) | `parent_uuid` | `String` | `Option<String>` | Can be `null` |
| `QueueOperationRecord` (line 480) | `content` | `Option<String>` | `Option<Value>` or enum | Can be a string or an array of content blocks |

> DEVELOPER

Overall LG, but I'm not sure we should be using the serde_json::Value, what if we created our own type, thoughts no changes!

> AGENT

Good thought. `Value` is a catch-all that loses type safety — we can't distinguish what shape those fields actually have, and it doesn't document the expected structure.

For the current uses:
- `thinkingMetadata` — has `level`, `disabled`, `triggers`
- `error` (system) — has `status`, `headers`, `requestID`
- `compactMetadata` — has `removedMessages` (probably more)
- `content` (queue-op) — either a string or array of content blocks
- `todos`, `toolUseResult`, etc. — various shapes

The tradeoff: typed structs are better documentation and catch schema changes via `deny_unknown_fields`, but they're more work per field and risk breakage on new sub-fields. `Value` is expedient but opaque.

A middle ground could be a newtype like `Opaque(Value)` that signals "we know this is structured but haven't typed it yet" — distinguishing it from fields we've deliberately modeled. Then we could incrementally promote `Opaque` fields to real structs as we learn their shapes.

But that's probably a separate chore, not part of this error-fixing sweep. For now `Value` gets us to zero errors. Want to file it as a future todo item?

> DEVELOPER

Agreed not here, but add that as a todo and doc in chores, and include this thought that if we choose a catch-all I'd lean towards our own type.

**Full path in error output** — easy win, we already have it. The `example_file` is just the filename, but we could store the full path instead. Should at least be available with `-e`.

$ ccs-viewer . -r --error-id a3f2
Files with error [a3f2]:
  /home/wink/data/prgs/foo/.claude/074c6334-....jsonl
  /home/wink/data/prgs/bar/.claude/abc123-....jsonl
  ...
```

The ID could be a short hash of the error message — deterministic across runs so you don't need to re-run `-e` first.

But that's a feature on its own, not part of the "fix errors" sweep. For dev3 I'd suggest:

1. Switch `example_file` to full path (trivial)
2. Add the error ID + drill-down as a future todo

Want to handle #1 in dev3 alongside the summary variant, and file #2 for later?

> DEVELOPER

But it would be useful for fixing dev3 so maybe dev2.1 we add this feature and maybe rather than having to rerun the app caches the output in some configurable but default location maybe in a /tmp/ccs-viewer-cache-<uuid>.json so we could get it on a subsequent run, just blue-sky thinking

> AGENT

Yeah, the cache idea is nice — run once, explore later. A flow like:

```
$ ccs-viewer . -r -e
Errors:
  [a3f2] 689x unknown variant `summary` ... (250 files)
  [b7c1] 4x expected value ... (1 file)

Summary: 2689 file(s), ... (cached: /tmp/ccs-viewer-cache-xxx.json)

$ ccs-viewer --error-id a3f2
689x unknown variant `summary` ...
Files:
  /home/wink/data/prgs/foo/.claude/074c6334-....jsonl:1
  /home/wink/data/prgs/bar/.claude/abc123-....jsonl:1
  ...
```

The cache could store per-error-ID: message, record type, and full list of `path:line` hits. The `--error-id` flag reads from the most recent cache (or a specified one).

But this is getting into real feature territory — cache format, lookup logic, staleness, cleanup. I think it's a distraction from the current error-fixing sweep.

Counter-proposal: for dev3 just switch to full paths in `-e` output and collect all files per error (not just one example). That gives you the drill-down without any caching infrastructure. The cache/ID system goes into a future todo.

```
Errors:
  689x unknown variant `summary` ... in summary (250 files):
    /home/wink/.../074c6334-....jsonl:1
    /home/wink/.../abc123-....jsonl:1
    ...
```

Maybe gated behind `-ee` (double errors) or `--error-files` to avoid flooding output. Thoughts?

> DEVELOPER

maybe -E list one error line followed by the full paths?

> AGENT

Clean. So:

- `-e`: current behavior (one-line summary per error group)
- `-E`: same one-line summary, followed by indented full paths with line numbers

```
$ ccs-viewer . -r -E
Errors:
  689x unknown variant `summary` ... in summary (250 files)
    /home/wink/.../074c6334-....jsonl:1
    /home/wink/.../abc123-....jsonl:1
    ...
  4x expected value ... in unknown (1 file)
    /home/wink/.../5d2dad0a-....jsonl:216

Summary: ...
```

Simple, no caching needed, builds on existing error groups. I'll do this as dev2.1 before dev3. The cache/ID idea goes to a future todo.

Sound right?

> DEVELOPER

y

> AGENT

This is a git stash conflict (`git stash pop` that conflicted). The three sections are:

1. **Lines 216-302** (`<<<<<<< Updated upstream`): the state that was already in the working tree when you popped
2. **Lines 303-304** (`||||||| Stash base` + `=======`): the common ancestor (empty — meaning the stash base had nothing at those lines)
3. **Lines 305-395** (`>>>>>>> Stashed changes`): the stashed changes you were trying to apply

Since this is a JSONL file, each line is an independent record. The resolution is straightforward: **keep both sides** — all the records from "Updated upstream" (216-302) AND all the records from "Stashed changes" (305-395), just remove the 4 conflict marker lines (216, 303, 304, 396).

The records don't overlap (different UUIDs, different timestamps), so it's a clean concatenation.

But — this is in `rust/rlibc-x/.claude/`, not our repo. So we shouldn't fix it as part of this ccs-viewer work. You'd want to resolve it in that project. The fix is just deleting those 4 lines:

```
line 216: <<<<<<< Updated upstream
line 303: ||||||| Stash base
line 304: =======
line 396: >>>>>>> Stashed changes
```

Want to note this in chores as a known data issue, or just leave it alone?

**File 2:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/rlibc-x/.claude/5d2dad0a-198a-4c08-b8c9-cb9dd3f02ded.jsonl` (line 484)
```json
{"type":"summary","summary":"Implement tspec CLI with ts list command","leafUuid":"81a21cdd-9bd5-4105-aa00-a12bf7a47ac8"}
```

Both summary records have the same shape -- three fields:
- `type` -- always `"summary"`
- `summary` -- a short text description of the session/conversation
- `leafUuid` -- a UUID reference (likely pointing to a message node in the conversation tree)

Notable: in the box-with-tri-hole file the summary is on line 1 (the very first record), while in the rlibc-x file it appears on line 484 (near the end). This suggests summary records can appear at different positions in a session file.

> AGENT

Push + finalize?

> DEVELOPER

Let's finalize this, but I think we need to tweak Summary.

> AGENT

What's the tweak you have in mind?

> DEVELOPER

So "files" in summary are the count of the files that we could convert properly, but I think we should have a "total" which is the sum of all the categories

> AGENT

Good point. So something like:

```
Summary: 871 file(s), 122271 records, 0 errors, 2 skipped, 16 empty (889 total)
```

Where `889 = 871 + 2 + 16`. That way you can sanity-check the numbers at a glance.

Or do you want total first?

```
Summary: 889 total, 871 file(s), 122271 records, 0 errors, 2 skipped, 16 empty
```

> DEVELOPER

Maybe
File Summary: 889 total, 871 valid, 2 skipped, 16 empty, 0 errors, 122271 records
  or
Summary: 122271 records, 871 valid, 2 skipped, 16 empty, 0 errors, 889 total files

What would you suggest the user knows that the records are what was processed in the valid fales and isn't the "total"

> AGENT

I'd go with your second form, slightly tweaked:

```
Summary: 889 files, 871 valid, 16 empty, 2 skipped, 0 errors, 122271 records
```

Reasoning:
- Start with the big picture (total files) then break it down
- `valid` immediately follows `files` — the natural question after "how many files" is "how many worked"
- `empty` and `skipped` are the "not valid" categories, grouped together
- `errors` and `records` are about what happened inside the valid files
- The user reads left-to-right: files → breakdown → results

The "total files" framing makes it obvious that `records` belongs to the valid files, not the total.

Thoughts?

> DEVELOPER

But in your tweak version you didn't sue "total files"?

> AGENT

You're right. Let me be explicit:

```
Summary: 889 total files, 871 valid, 16 empty, 2 skipped, 0 errors, 122271 records
```

That makes it unambiguous that `records` relates to the valid files, not total files.

> DEVELOPER

How about:
Summary: 889 total files, 871 valid files with 122271 records, 16 empty, 2 skipped, 0 errors

Maybe to verbose but no ambiguity

> AGENT

I like it — reads naturally left to right and `with` ties records to valid files unambiguously. Not too verbose for a one-line summary. Let's go with that. Fold into dev3.1 before pushing?

> DEVELOPER

y

I didn't finish, sorry let me try again the flag matches the summary but probably no -t(?) and lower case give a file listing consistently and -E is the "deduplicated" to make --errors list verbose, although maybe drop the deduplication as generally we should have only minimal number of errors now.

> AGENT

Let me see if I understand. The flags mirror the summary fields:

| Summary field | Flag | Shows |
|--------------|------|-------|
| **t**otal files | (none, always shown) | — |
| **v**alid files | `-v` | per-file detail lines |
| **e**mpty | `-e` | list of empty files |
| **s**kipped | `-s` | list of skipped files |
| **E**rrors | `-E` | error details with full paths |
| **r**ecords | (shown in summary) | — |

So `-e` moves from errors to empty, and errors become `-E` only (with full paths, no deduplication since error counts should be small now).

That's a clean mental model — lowercase flags list files in that category, `-E` gives the error detail view.

But this is a significant CLI rework. Should we do it as a separate step (dev3.2 or maybe even 0.13.0) rather than squeezing it into the 0.12.0 release?

| `-s` | skipped file paths |

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/main.rs",
  "old_string": "    let show_errors = cli.errors || cli.error_files;\n    if show_errors && !error_groups.is_empty() {\n        println!(\"{}Errors:\", if cli.list { \"\\n\" } else { \"\" });\n        let mut groups: Vec<_> = error_groups.into_iter().collect();\n        groups.sort_by(|a, b| b.1.count.cmp(&a.1.count));\n        for (key, group) in &groups {\n            let first = &group.hits[0];\n            println!(\n                \"  {}x {} in {} ({}:{} in {} file(s))\",\n                group.count, key.message, key.record_type, first.path, first.line, group.file_count,\n            );\n            if cli.error_files {\n                for hit in &group.hits {\n                    println!(\"    {}:{}\", hit.path, hit.line);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        println!();\n    }",
  "new_string": "    let show_errors = cli.errors || cli.error_summary;\n    if show_errors && !error_groups.is_empty() {\n        println!(\"{}Errors:\", if cli.list { \"\\n\" } else { \"\" });\n        if cli.error_summary {\n            // -E: deduplicated summary grouped by message\n            let mut groups: Vec<_> = error_groups.into_iter().collect();\n            groups.sort_by(|a, b| b.1.count.cmp(&a.1.count));\n            for (key, group) in &groups {\n                let first = &group.hits[0];\n                println!(\n                    \"  {}x {} in {} ({}:{} in {} file(s))\",\n                    group.count, key.message, key.record_type, first.path, first.line,\n                    group.file_count,\n                );\n            }\n        } else {\n            // -e: flat list of all error file:line paths\n            let mut all_hits: Vec<_> = error_groups\n                .values()\n                .flat_map(|g| g.hits.iter())\n                .collect();\n            all_hits.sort_by(|a, b| a.path.cmp(&b.path).then(a.line.cmp(&b.line)));\n            for hit in all_hits {\n                println!(\"  {}:{}\", hit.path, hit.line);\n            }\n        }\n        println!();\n    }"
}

```
Claude Code session JSONL viewer

Usage: ccs-viewer [OPTIONS] <PATTERNS>...

Arguments:
  <PATTERNS>...  File or directory glob patterns to process

Options:
  -l, --list           Show per-file summary lines (one line per file with record type counts)
  -e, --errors         Show error file paths with line numbers
  -E, --error-summary  Show deduplicated error summary (grouped by message, sorted by count)
  -r, --recursive      Recursively search directories for matching files
      --glob <GLOBS>   File glob pattern for recursive mode (repeatable, default: *.jsonl, agent-*.meta.json)
      --strict         Exit 2 if deserialization errors are present
  -s, --skipped        Show files skipped by the first-line sniff test
  -z, --zero           Show zero-length files
  -h, --help           Print help
  -V, --version        Print version

Output order: per-file list (-l) > errors (-e/-E) > skipped (-s) > zero-len (-z) > summary (always last).

Summary: <total> total files, <valid> valid files with <records> records[, <n> zero-len][, <n> skipped][, <n> errors]
  total:    all files found by glob/recursive search
  valid:    files successfully processed (total minus zero-len and skipped)
  records:  total successfully deserialized records (in valid files)
  zero-len: zero-length files (shown when non-zero)
  skipped:  files that failed the first-line sniff test (shown when non-zero)
  errors:   total deserialization failures (shown when non-zero)

Exit codes:
  0  Success (default, even with deserialization errors)
  1  Tool failure (bad args, can't open file, no files match)
  2  Deserialization errors present (only with --strict)
```

Arguments:

  -r, --recursive     Recursively search directories for matching files
      --glob <GLOBS>  File glob pattern for recursive mode (repeatable, default: *.jsonl, agent-*.meta.json)
      --strict        Exit 2 if deserialization errors are present

  -V, --version       Print version

  -v, --valid          Show valid files (one line per file with record type counts)

  -s, --skipped        Show files skipped by the first-line sniff test
  -z, --zero           Show zero-length files

Summary: <total> total files, <valid> valid files with <records> records[, <n> zero-len][, <n> skipped][, <n> errors]
  total:    all files found by glob/recursive search
  valid:    files successfully processed (total minus zero-len and skipped)
  records:  total successfully deserialized records (in valid files)
  zero-len: zero-length files (shown when non-zero)
  skipped:  files that failed the first-line sniff test (shown when non-zero)
  errors:   total deserialization failures (shown when non-zero)

Exit codes:
  0  Success (default, even with deserialization errors)
  1  Tool failure (bad args, can't open file, no files match)
  2  Deserialization errors present (only with --strict)

Usage: ccs-viewer [OPTIONS] <PATTERNS>...

Arguments:
  <PATTERNS>...  File or directory glob patterns to process

Options:
  -r, --recursive     Recursively search directories for matching files
      --glob <GLOBS>  File glob pattern for recursive mode (repeatable, default: *.jsonl, agent-*.meta.json)
      --strict        Exit 2 if deserialization errors are present
  -h, --help          Print help
  -V, --version       Print version

Summary detail flags:
  -v, --valid          Show valid files (one line per file with record type counts)
  -e, --errors         Show error file paths with line numbers
  -E, --error-summary  Show deduplicated error summary (grouped by message, sorted by count)
  -s, --skipped        Show files skipped by the first-line sniff test
  -z, --zero           Show zero-length files

Output is always in this fixed order regardless of flag order:
  valid (-v) > errors (-e/-E) > skipped (-s) > zero-len (-z) > summary (always last)

Summary: <total> total files, <valid> valid files with <records> records[, <n> zero-len][, <n> skipped][, <n> errors]
  total:    all files found by glob/recursive search
  valid:    files successfully processed (total minus zero-len and skipped)
  records:  total successfully deserialized records (in valid files)
  zero-len: zero-length files (shown when non-zero)
  skipped:  files that failed the first-line sniff test (shown when non-zero)
  errors:   total deserialization failures (shown when non-zero)

Exit codes:
  0  Success (default, even with deserialization errors)
  1  Tool failure (bad args, can't open file, no files match)
  2  Deserialization errors present (only with --strict)
